OffOn

Section

LVAortic valveMitral valve

Similar studies, k 50

SectionLeft ventricle

Measurements6

Anchorcalipers kept

LVEF62%

**Left ventricle: all six calipers lie in their heads' ranges.** Each value stays as measured.

### Models that read echocardiograms

The first deep learning models for echocardiography each did one task, alone[50](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-50) or in a pipeline[51](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-51); later models learned many tasks at once from whole videos[52](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-52). A vision–language model also learns from the cardiologist's report: EchoCLIP, trained on videos and the text of their reports[53](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-53), reads one image at a time and up to 77 tokens of text[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54). EchoPrime reads every video of a study and up to 512 tokens of its report.

### How EchoPrime reads a study

EchoPrime has four parts:

- **View classifier.** It sorts B-mode and color Doppler videos into 58 standard views, which the public code groups into 11 view groups.
- **Video encoder.** A video transformer (mViT) turns each video, 16 frames of 224 × 224 pixels, into an embedding of 512 numbers.
- **Text encoder.** A language model (PubMedBERT), trained with the video encoder, turns a report into an embedding close to those of its study's videos.
- **Anatomical attention.** For each of the 15 report sections, each view group has a weight, learned by multiple instance learning; the public code holds the 165 weights. A section's study embedding is the normalized sum of the video embeddings, each multiplied by its view group's weight[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54).

With a single apical four-chamber video, EchoPrime performed across all tasks about as well as with full studies[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54): the weights show how it mixes the videos it has, not which videos a finding needs.

### Retrieval and task heads

The published model drafts each section by retrieval from the earlier studies whose embeddings lie closest to the study's own. A number, such as the LVEF, is the mean of the 50 closest; a finding enters the report when more than half of them carry it[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54). A rare finding is therefore found only when similar studies are in the library. Recent versions, in development and not yet published, replace retrieval with about 150 task heads, one for each task of the report. Their number heads anchor the measurements and adjust them.

### How EchoNet-Measurements measures

Earlier, EchoNet-Dynamic estimated the LVEF over several beats[55](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-55), and EchoNet-LVH measured LV wall thickness and diameter[56](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-56). EchoNet-Measurements, trained on measurements made in routine practice, has a separate segmentation model for each of 18 measurements, nine in B-mode and nine in Doppler and M-mode[57](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-57). Each model measures one thing in the view it expects.

A model outputs a heat map for each point it looks for: two for a caliper, its two ends, and one for a Doppler peak. In the public code, each point is the weighted center of its heat map. The calibration that the DICOM file stores converts pixels to centimeters or velocities. An export to a common video format drops the calibration: the points remain, but the model cannot give a number.

### What the figure shows

The figure follows one study of the heart shown through both models to one report, in seven steps. A click on the strip of sections, a tile or a sentence chooses a section.

The task heads and the anchor are a simulation. Each head's value is the site's own measurement of the heart shown, and its range is that value ± 10%: a band chosen for the figure, not an error of the model. Outside the range, the caliper's end moves to the heat map's peak that agrees with the head; with no such peak, the value is flagged for review. The images and the words of the report are the site's own, and the heat maps are drawn on the site's own caliper points. Neither model processed these images.

### Performance

Across 17 classification and 11 regression tasks, the mean AUC of EchoPrime was 0.92 at Cedars-Sinai and 0.85 to 0.89 at the external sites. The mean absolute error of the LVEF was 4.79 percentage points in the internal test set and 4.14 to 6.46 at the external sites, against 6.23 to 10.37 for EchoCLIP[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54).

EchoNet-Measurements was compared with sonographers by the coverage probability: the share of paired measurements that differ by less than an acceptable difference. Across the 18 measurements, the mean coverage probability was 0.796 in held-out studies at Cedars-Sinai and 0.839 in external studies at Stanford Healthcare[57](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-57).

### Limits

- Each model was trained on the studies of one center, and all the data of EchoPrime were collected retrospectively[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54). EchoNet-Measurements learns the conventions and the errors of its center's sonographers.
- Spectral Doppler and M-mode recordings are still images, so EchoPrime was not trained on them; the authors point to models that measure them directly[54](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-54), [57](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-57).
- On a poor image, a sonographer can use the clinical context and the frames around it; a measurement model, which reads a single image, cannot[57](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-57). The 18 measurements do not yet include the velocity time integral, the PISA radius or the LA volume[57](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-57).

### Prospective trials

In the first blinded randomized trial of AI in echocardiography, a cardiologist, blind to the source, finalized an initial LVEF from AI or from a sonographer. The cardiologist changed the LVEF by more than 5% in 16.8% of the AI group and 27.2% of the sonographer group; AI was non-inferior and superior[58](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-58).

The AI ECHO INSIGHT trial tests whole reports: a blinded cardiologist finalizes a preliminary report drafted by AI, by a cardiologist or by a sonographer, and the primary outcome is the rate of substantial change[59](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-59). The results are not yet published.

### Key points

1. EchoPrime sorts each video by view, turns it into 512 numbers and weights it by view group for each of the 15 report sections.
2. The published model drafts each section from the 50 closest earlier studies; the weights show how it mixes views, not which views a finding needs.
3. EchoNet-Measurements has one model for each of 18 measurements. Each point is the weighted center of a heat map, and the calibration stored in the DICOM file converts pixels to centimeters or velocities.
4. In a blinded randomized trial, cardiologists changed an AI-first LVEF less often than a sonographer-first LVEF.

## Left ventricular assist devices

The HeartMate 3: the surveillance study, the speed ramp and complications

A continuous-flow LVAD takes blood from the LV apex through an inflow cannula and returns it to the ascending aorta through an outflow graft. Echocardiography shows how far the pump unloads the LV at each speed, and it detects the complications of the device.

Pump speed 5,300 rpm

rightwardneutralleftwardsuction

Case

Working pumpRV failureDe novo ARObstruction

Pump speed5,300rpm

LVIDd5.0cm

Aortic valveintermittent1 of 3 · 110 ms

Septumneutral

MRmildVC 0.18 cm

ARnone

Inflow cannula0.8m/s

Outflow graft1.3m/s

RVOT VTI16cm

Cardiac output4.3L/min

**A working pump: the LV is unloaded.** At 5,300 rpm the LV internal diameter at end-diastole is 5.0 cm. The aortic valve opens in 1 of 3 beats, for 110 ms, and the septum is neutral[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). MR is mild (vena contracta 0.18 cm)[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). TR is mild (vena contracta 0.20 cm)[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29), and the RV basal diameter is 3.6 cm (normal)[32](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-32). The inflow cannula's PW peak is 0.8 m/s, and 1.1 m/s with the artificial pulse[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60). The outflow graft's CW peak is 1.3 m/s, below 2 m/s[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60). The RVOT VTI is 16 cm, a total cardiac output of 4.3 L/min[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). The LV narrows by 0.15 cm per 100 rpm[62](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-62).

### The device

Echocardiography shows the inflow cannula and parts of the outflow graft, but not the impeller[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60). The HeartMate 3 (HM3), now the only LVAD implanted, typically runs at 4,800 to 5,600 rpm[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60).

Every 2 seconds the HM3 lowers its speed by 2,000 rpm for 0.15 s and then raises it by 4,000 rpm for 0.20 s. This artificial pulse makes a Doppler peak out of step with the heartbeat, not to be mistaken for intermittent obstruction or for opening of the aortic valve[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60). The inflow usually peaks below 1.0 m/s, up to 1.2 m/s with the pulse; the outflow graft peaks below 2 m/s on CW Doppler, but the pulse can take it above 2 m/s[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60).

### The surveillance study

A surveillance study should be considered about 2 weeks after implantation or before discharge, whichever comes first, then at 1, 3, 6 and 12 months, and every 6 to 12 months after that[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).

- **LV size.** The LV internal diameter at end-diastole (LVIDd) in the parasternal long axis is the most reproducible measure of the LV. By 3 months it can be expected to fall by at least 15% from its value before implantation[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
- **Aortic valve.** M-mode shows whether the valve opens[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). A valve that stays closed is linked to thrombus in the aortic root and to de novo AR[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
- **Septum.** At end-diastole, a leftward shift of the septum comes from a high RV end-diastolic pressure, a low LV preload or too high a speed; a rightward shift, from a high LV end-diastolic pressure: too low a speed, pump dysfunction, severe AR or a high afterload[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
- **Inflow cannula.** A well-placed cannula lies at or near the LV apex and points at the mitral valve, with laminar flow into it[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
- **Cardiac output.** When the aortic valve stays closed and there is no significant AR or PR, the total cardiac output from the RVOT VTI ( [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure)) equals the LVAD's output[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).

### The speed ramp

A ramp study records the echocardiogram at each step of speed: in the HM3 studies, from 4,600 rpm in steps of 100 rpm to 6,200 rpm[62](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-62), [63](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-63). Because a lower speed can open the aortic valve over a root thrombus and cause an embolism, the study starts after a check of the anticoagulation and of the baseline images for thrombus[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).

As the speed of a working pump rises, the LV gets smaller, the aortic valve opens less often or for less time and the RVOT stroke volume rises[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). With the HM3, the LVEDD fell by 0.15 cm for each 100 rpm[62](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-62), and the RV volumes rose only at the higher speeds[64](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-64).

Below the minimum speed, the LVIDd rises from baseline, the septum may shift rightward, the MR may grow and the aortic valve may open more often or for longer. Above the maximum speed, the septum shifts leftward or impedes the flow into the cannula, and the TR may worsen[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). The optimal HM3 speed is 100 rpm below the maximum speed of optimal LV unloading[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60), and the opening of the aortic valve is a secondary goal to the relief of heart failure[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60).

A suction event, a partial occlusion of the inflow cannula by a segment of LV myocardium, usually comes from over-pumping, and a lower speed often corrects it; a low preload or a low afterload, as in hypovolemia or sepsis, can also cause it[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). Its signs are an LV smaller than about 3 cm, a leftward shift of the septum and worse TR, with or without ventricular ectopy and intermittent obstruction of the inflow[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). An inflow velocity above 1.5 m/s on CW Doppler points to an obstruction of the inflow, from suction or a malpositioned cannula[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).

### RV failure

RV failure on LVAD support shows as a larger RV with lower systolic function, a dilated IVC, more TR, a lower RVOT stroke volume, and lower velocities in the cannula and the graft, below 0.5 m/s in severe failure[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). A septum bowed far to the left, with a small LV and a dilated, failing RV, means that the pump lacks preload, and the speed should be lowered[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60). The RV basal diameter is graded as in [chapter 11](https://intro2echo.com/rv): normal below 4.1 cm, and severely dilated above 4.9 cm[32](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-32).

### Aortic regurgitation

De novo AR occurs in about 25% to 33% of patients by 12 months and affects the pump's performance, morbidity and mortality[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). The pump sends the regurgitant blood back to the aorta, a blind loop: the flow in the device is high, but the forward output, the RVOT cardiac output, falls and the LV grows[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). Higher speeds tend to increase the AR[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60).

A vena contracta of 0.3 cm or more, or a jet width of more than 46% of the LVOT at a Nyquist limit of 50 to 60 cm/s, indicates at least moderate AR. The pressure half-time and the diastolic flow reversal in the aorta are not reliable on LVAD support, because the AR runs into systole, and with the valve shut it is continuous[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61) ( [chapter 13](https://intro2echo.com/ar)).

### Obstruction

A thrombus in the pump or an obstruction of the cannula or the graft reduces the pump's flow. Against the patient's baseline, the LV dilates again, the septum shifts rightward, the aortic valve opens more and the diastolic velocities in the cannula and the graft fall[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). When the speed is raised, the LVIDd, the opening of the aortic valve and the RVOT stroke volume do not change as expected[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61). Near an obstruction of the outflow graft the peak velocity is 2 m/s or more[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61); the figure samples the graft at the narrowing.

### Key points

1. With a working HM3, the LV gets smaller by 0.15 cm per 100 rpm[62](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-62). An LV below 3 cm with a leftward septum is a suction event[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
2. The Doppler peak of the HM3's artificial pulse, every 2 seconds, is neither an obstruction nor an opening of the aortic valve[60](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-60).
3. In de novo AR the pump flow is high while the RVOT output falls; a vena contracta of 0.3 cm or more indicates at least moderate AR[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).
4. An LV, an aortic valve and an RVOT output that do not change with the speed point to an obstruction[61](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-61).

12

## Aortic stenosis

LV pressure load

In calcific aortic stenosis, the valve orifice narrows, the transvalvular jet velocity increases and the LV wall thickens in response to the pressure load. In the late stage, LV systolic dysfunction reduces the stroke volume, and the transvalvular gradient decreases with it.

**Severe aortic stenosis.** The AVA by the continuity equation is 0.70 cm², the peak velocity is 4.9 m/s and the mean gradient is 56 mmHg[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). The LV wall thickens in response to the pressure load (septum 13 mm, LV mass index 110 g/m²)[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). The average E/e′ is 14.

### Natural history

1. In **progressive stenosis**, mild stenosis has a peak velocity of 2.0–2.9 m/s or a mean gradient below 20 mmHg, and moderate stenosis has a peak velocity of 3.0–3.9 m/s or a mean gradient of 20–39 mmHg. Early LV diastolic dysfunction can be present, and the LVEF is normal[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
2. **Severe stenosis** is defined by a peak velocity ≥4 m/s or a mean gradient ≥40 mmHg, and the valve area is typically ≤1.0 cm². The LV has diastolic dysfunction and mild hypertrophy, with a normal LVEF[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). Very severe stenosis is defined by a peak velocity ≥5 m/s or a mean gradient ≥60 mmHg[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
3. **LV dysfunction** in severe stenosis is defined by an LVEF below 50%[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
4. In **low-flow, low-gradient stenosis**, LV systolic dysfunction reduces the transvalvular flow; the area is ≤1.0 cm², with a peak velocity below 4 m/s or a mean gradient below 40 mmHg[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

The ACC/AHA stages also require the symptom status: patients in stage C have no symptoms, and patients in stage D have symptoms[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). The figure and the diagram model the echocardiographic findings only, and the diagram names the echocardiographic pattern, not the stage.

### Grading

AVA = LVOT area × VTI(LVOT) ÷ VTI(AV)  ·  ΔP = 4v²  ·  mean gradient = mean of 4v² over ejection

Chapter [07 Doppler hemodynamics](https://intro2echo.com/pressure) describes the stroke volume from the LVOT and the simplified Bernoulli equation. The stroke volume that crosses the LVOT also crosses the valve. The valve area therefore equals the LVOT area × the LVOT VTI ÷ the AV VTI, the continuity equation[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). The diagram grades each criterion by Table 3 of the EACVI/ASE recommendations[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). Table 3 grades a peak velocity of 2.5 m/s or less as aortic sclerosis without stenosis[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). The ACC/AHA progressive stage starts at 2.0 m/s[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

- The highest jet velocity should be recorded with continuous-wave (CW) Doppler from several acoustic windows, and the window that gives it should be reported; angle correction should not be used[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30) ( [chapter 03](https://intro2echo.com/shift)).
- The peak gradient is 4 × (peak velocity)². The mean gradient is the average of the instantaneous gradients over ejection, from the traced CW envelope[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30).
- A peak velocity ≥4.0 m/s, a mean gradient ≥40 mmHg or an area <1.0 cm² each suggests severe stenosis; ideally, all three criteria are concordant[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). A velocity ratio below 0.25 also indicates severe stenosis[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30).
- The dimensionless index is the ratio of the LVOT VTI to the AV VTI. The velocity ratio is the ratio of the LVOT peak velocity to the AV peak velocity. Neither uses the LVOT diameter. The diagram grades both by the velocity-ratio cut-offs of Table 3.
- As the obstruction becomes more severe, the velocity peaks later in systole and the Doppler envelope becomes more rounded; in mild stenosis, the velocity peaks early and the envelope is triangular[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30).

In the diagram above, a change in one measurement moves the slider to a band of the new result. A move of the slider returns every value to the model. The slider has no band for paradoxical low-flow, low-gradient stenosis: in the model, the LVEF falls before the gradient does.

### Low-flow, low-gradient aortic stenosis

A high gradient is a peak velocity ≥4 m/s or a mean gradient ≥40 mmHg. It indicates severe stenosis whether the flow and the LV function are normal or reduced[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). With an area <1.0 cm² and a low gradient, the flow and the LVEF name the pattern[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). Low flow is defined as a stroke volume index below 35 mL/m²[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30).

- **Classical low-flow, low-gradient stenosis** has an LVEF <50%[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). During low-dose dobutamine echocardiography, a velocity ≥4.0 m/s with an area that remains ≤1.0 cm² confirms severe stenosis, whereas an increase in area to above 1.0 cm² suggests pseudo-severe stenosis[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). Stage D2 also requires symptoms and severe stenosis confirmed by dobutamine echocardiography[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
- In **paradoxical low-flow, low-gradient stenosis**, the LVEF is ≥50%. A small LV with a low stroke volume produces a low gradient across a severe stenosis[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). Stage D3 also requires symptoms[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
- **Normal-flow, low-gradient stenosis** has an LVEF ≥50% and a stroke volume index ≥35 mL/m²[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). It often reflects an error of measurement, most often an LVOT diameter that is measured too small[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30). At normal flow, a mean gradient of 40 mmHg corresponds to an area nearer 0.8 than 1.0 cm²[30](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-30).

### Key points

1. The thresholds for severe aortic stenosis are a peak velocity of 4 m/s, a mean gradient of 40 mmHg and a valve area of 1.0 cm²; ideally, all three criteria are concordant.
2. The continuity equation squares an error in the LVOT diameter. The dimensionless index and the velocity ratio do not use the diameter.
3. LV wall thickening develops first, and the LVEF falls only late in the course of the disease.
4. A low gradient with an area below 1.0 cm² needs an assessment of the flow and the LVEF.

13

## Aortic regurgitation

LV volume and pressure load

In aortic regurgitation, blood flows back from the aorta through the incompetent aortic valve into the LV during diastole. The LV receives blood from both the LA and the aorta, dilates and ejects a large stroke volume. In the late stage, LV systolic dysfunction develops.

Severity moderate

**Moderate aortic regurgitation.** The regurgitant volume is 55 mL, the regurgitant fraction 47% and the EROA 0.32 cm²; the jet width is 55% of the LVOT diameter, and the pressure half-time is 267 ms. The specific criteria do not decide the grade (mild 1 of 6, severe 0 of 7), so the quantitative grade III decides[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The LV receives and ejects the forward and the regurgitant volume (LVOT VTI 34.1 cm, LV end-diastolic diameter 57 mm); the blood pressure is 134/64 mmHg.

Figure 25 gives no number for five criteria. The flow convergence is the observer’s call; the model calls it small below a regurgitant volume of 30 mL and large from 60 mL, where its other signs cross their cut-offs. A soft CW jet is a jet that the figure draws faint, at a regurgitant volume below 30 mL. A normal LV size uses the upper limit for men[14](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-14). Normal LV function is an LVEF above 55%, because an LVEF ≤55% is LV dysfunction in AR[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). ### Grading and staging

The pressure half-time is the time in which the diastolic pressure difference between the aorta and the LV falls by half; a more severe leak equalizes the two pressures faster and shortens it[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29) ( [chapter 14](https://intro2echo.com/ms) measures it at the mitral valve).

1. **Mild** aortic regurgitation is characterized by a small central jet, a faint or incomplete CW Doppler signal, a pressure half-time above 500 ms and brief early diastolic flow reversal in the descending aorta[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
2. In **moderate** aortic regurgitation, the LV is normal in size or dilated, the pressure half-time is 500–200 ms and the regurgitant volume is 30–59 mL[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
3. **Severe** aortic regurgitation is indicated by a vena contracta >0.6 cm, a jet width ≥65% of the LVOT diameter, a regurgitant volume ≥60 mL, a regurgitant fraction ≥50%, an EROA ≥0.30 cm², a pressure half-time below 200 ms and holodiastolic flow reversal in the descending aorta[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Holodiastolic flow reversal in the abdominal aorta is consistent with severe AR[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The EACVI uses an end-diastolic velocity ≥20 cm/s in the proximal descending aorta as a criterion of severe AR[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65). The diagnosis of chronic severe AR also requires LV dilation[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
4. In asymptomatic severe AR, ACC/AHA stage C1 has an LVEF >55% and an LV end-systolic diameter ≤50 mm; stage C2, with **LV dysfunction**, has an LVEF ≤55%, or an end-systolic diameter >50 mm or >25 mm/m²[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

### Integrated grading

The ASE algorithm first counts the specific criteria. Four or more specific criteria for mild AR define mild regurgitation, and four or more for severe AR define severe regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Otherwise, the regurgitant volume, the regurgitant fraction and the EROA assign a grade from I to IV. Grade I is mild, grades II and III are moderate, and grade IV is severe[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Grade III with three specific criteria for severe AR also indicates severe regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). If the findings are inconsistent, the grade is indeterminate, and TEE or CMR can quantify the regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The algorithm gives no rule for discordant quantitative measures; the diagram uses the middle grade of the three.

### LVOT velocity in aortic regurgitation

The LV ejects the forward stroke volume plus the volume that returns to the LV in diastole. The regurgitant volume is the stroke volume through the regurgitant valve minus the stroke volume through a competent valve[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29), each calculated as in [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure). In the figure, the LVOT VTI increases as the regurgitation becomes more severe. The diagram compares the LVOT with the mitral stroke volume, which is valid without significant MR, and divides the regurgitant volume by the VTI of the AR jet to give the EROA[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).

### Pitfalls

- The pressure half-time shortens as the LV diastolic pressure rises, and it can lengthen with chronic adaptation to severe AR[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
- The length of the color Doppler jet in the LV is not a reliable measure of severity[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).

### Key points

1. Four or more specific criteria for mild or for severe AR define the grade; otherwise, the regurgitant volume, the regurgitant fraction and the EROA decide.
2. Severe aortic regurgitation is indicated by a vena contracta >0.6 cm, a jet width ≥65% of the LVOT diameter and a regurgitant volume ≥60 mL.
3. Holodiastolic flow reversal in the abdominal aorta indicates severe aortic regurgitation.
4. In asymptomatic severe AR, an LVEF ≤55% or an LV end-systolic diameter >50 mm defines ACC/AHA stage C2.

14

## Mitral stenosis

LV inflow obstruction

In rheumatic mitral stenosis, the commissures fuse and the leaflets dome in diastole. As the mitral orifice narrows, LA pressure increases, the LA enlarges and pulmonary artery pressure rises, with secondary involvement of the right heart.

Severity severe

nonemildmoderatesevere

Heart rate 70 /min

Mitral valve area1.36cm²

Pressure half-time162ms

Mean gradient7.9mmHg

PA systolic pressure54mmHg

LA volume index48mL/m²

**Severe mitral stenosis.** The valve area by planimetry is 1.36 cm²[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66). The pressure half-time is 162 ms (an area of 1.36 cm² by 220 ÷ PHT), and at a heart rate of 70 beats per minute the mean gradient is 7.9 mmHg. The LA volume index increases to 48 mL/m² and the PA systolic pressure to 54 mmHg; the ACC/AHA guideline lists a PA systolic pressure above 50 mmHg as a consequence of severe stenosis[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). The mean gradient lies in the moderate band; when the measures disagree, stress echocardiography can assess the hemodynamic severity[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66).

Planimetry is the preferred method for the valve area[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66), so the area gives the grade here. A normal area is 4.0–5.0 cm²[67](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-67); from 4.0 cm² the diagram calls the valve normal. The bands of the mean gradient hold at a heart rate of 60–80/min[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66).

Agreement among the measures makes the grade more reliable. When they disagree, stress echocardiography can assess the hemodynamic severity[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66).

### Echocardiographic findings by stage

1. In **progressive stenosis**, the commissures are fused and the leaflets dome in diastole; the valve area is >1.5 cm², the pressure half-time is <150 ms, LA enlargement is mild to moderate and pulmonary artery pressure is normal at rest[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
2. In **severe stenosis**, the valve area is ≤1.5 cm² and the pressure half-time is ≥150 ms; LA enlargement is severe, and the PA systolic pressure is above 50 mmHg[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

The ASE divides progressive stenosis into mild stenosis (valve area >2.5 cm², pressure half-time <100 ms, mean gradient <5 mmHg, PA systolic pressure <30 mmHg) and moderate stenosis (valve area 2.5–1.6 cm², pressure half-time 100–149 ms, mean gradient 5–9 mmHg, PA systolic pressure 30–49 mmHg)[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66).

### Grading

The ASE grades mitral stenosis with several measures: the valve area, the pressure half-time, the mean gradient and the PA systolic pressure. Agreement among them makes the grade more reliable, and when they disagree, stress echocardiography can assess the hemodynamic severity[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66). Planimetry is the preferred method for the valve area[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66), so the diagram grades by the planimetered area and shows whether each other measure lies in the same band.

The PA systolic pressure is estimated as the TR gradient plus the RA pressure ( [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure)); the figure sets the RA pressure at 3 mmHg, and at 8 mmHg in severe stenosis, and in a patient the IVC gives it ( [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure)).

### Measurement of valve area and gradient

MVA = 220 ÷ pressure half-time

- **Planimetry** of the mitral orifice is performed in the parasternal short-axis view, which also displays the commissural fusion[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). When the methods give discordant areas, planimetry is the reference method[67](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-67).
- The **pressure half-time** is the time required for the peak transmitral pressure gradient to fall by half; at that time, the velocity has fallen to about 70% of its peak[66](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-66), [9](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-9). The constant 220 is empirical[67](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-67); Hatle described the method in 1979[68](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-68). By this equation, a pressure half-time of 100 ms corresponds to an area of 2.2 cm² and 150 ms to 1.47 cm², so near these values the area and the half-time can lie in different bands. The pressure half-time is not reliable immediately after balloon valvotomy or in the presence of severe aortic regurgitation or abnormal LV compliance[67](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-67).
- The **mean gradient**, the average of the instantaneous gradients ( [chapter 12](https://intro2echo.com/as)), varies with heart rate and transmitral flow, so it is not a criterion of severity; it should be reported together with the heart rate[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38). In the figure, the stroke volume falls as the heart rate rises, and the cardiac output stays at about 4.9 L/min. At 110 beats per minute, the diastolic filling period is shorter, and the mean gradient across a severe stenosis about doubles. At 70 beats per minute, an area of 1.5 cm² gives a mean gradient of 6.6 mmHg, in the moderate band.
- During **exercise**, the stroke volume remains unchanged, and the cardiac output increases with the heart rate alone[69](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-69). In 10 patients, supine exercise increased the heart rate from 74 to 110 beats per minute and the mean gradient from 9 to 18 mmHg[70](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-70). The pressure half-time decreased from 225 to 190 ms, so the area by 220 ÷ PHT increased from 1.0 to 1.2 cm²; at high heart rates and flows the pressure half-time is less reliable[70](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-70).

### Key points

1. Mitral stenosis is severe when the valve area is ≤1.5 cm² and the pressure half-time is ≥150 ms.
2. Planimetry is the preferred method for the valve area; the pressure half-time, the mean gradient and the PA systolic pressure support the grade.
3. The hemodynamic consequences of mitral stenosis are LA enlargement and a rise in pulmonary artery pressure.
4. The mean gradient should be reported together with the heart rate at which it is measured.

15

### Echocardiographic findings by grade and stage

1. A small central jet, normal leaflets, a vena contracta below 0.3 cm, absent flow convergence and A-wave-dominant mitral inflow indicate **mild** regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
2. In **moderate** regurgitation, the LV and LA are normal in size or mildly dilated, and pulmonary vein flow is normal or shows systolic blunting[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
3. In **severe** regurgitation, the EROA is ≥0.40 cm², the regurgitant volume ≥60 mL, the regurgitant fraction ≥50% and the vena contracta ≥0.7 cm[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The LV and LA are dilated, the CW Doppler signal is dense and triangular, systolic flow in the pulmonary veins is absent or reversed, and the mitral E velocity exceeds 1.2 m/s[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
4. In the ACC/AHA classification, stage C1 is defined by an LVEF >60% and an LV end-systolic diameter <40 mm, and stage C2, with **LV dysfunction**, by an LVEF ≤60% or an LV end-systolic diameter ≥40 mm[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

### Integrated grading

The ASE algorithm first counts the specific criteria. Four or more specific criteria for mild MR define mild regurgitation, and four or more for severe MR define severe regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Otherwise, the EROA, the regurgitant volume and the regurgitant fraction assign a grade from I to IV. Grade I is mild, grades II and III are moderate, and grade IV is severe[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Grade III with three specific criteria for severe MR, or with an elliptical orifice, also indicates severe regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). If the findings are inconsistent, the grade is indeterminate, and TEE or CMR can quantify the regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The algorithm gives no rule for discordant quantitative measures; the diagram uses the middle grade of the three.

### PISA method

flow = 2π r² × Va  ·  EROA = flow ÷ peak velocity  ·  RVol = EROA × VTI

Blood accelerates as it converges on the regurgitant orifice; at the aliasing velocity (Va), the color changes, and the isovelocity surface at Va is a hemisphere of radius r[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The color baseline should be shifted toward the jet to lower Va to about 30 to 40 cm/s, and the radius should be measured at the time of the peak jet velocity[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). At this setting, a radius of 1.0 cm or more is specific for severe MR[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The method applies the principle of [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure): the flow rate through a surface equals its area × the velocity.

The figure applies the method in four steps:

1. The color scale is set so that the flow aliases at 32 cm/s. In a study, the view is zoomed on the mitral valve, and the color baseline is shifted toward the jet.
2. The radius r runs from the orifice to the first aliasing boundary. The flow rate is 2πr² × 32 cm/s.
3. The flow rate divided by the peak MR velocity of 5.0 m/s gives the EROA.
4. The EROA multiplied by the VTI of the jet gives the regurgitant volume. A jet through all of systole has a VTI of 150 cm.

For example, an EROA of 0.40 cm² at a peak velocity of 5.0 m/s corresponds to a flow rate of 200 mL/s. At 32 cm/s, this flow rate gives a radius of 1.00 cm, and the regurgitant volume is 60 mL. All three values reach the thresholds for severe MR[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). At the same velocity, an EROA of 0.10 cm² gives a radius of 0.50 cm, larger than the 0.3 cm of a small flow convergence in mild MR[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).

- The radius is squared in the flow equation; errors of 10 to 25% are common, even among experts[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
- An elliptical orifice, which is common in secondary MR, causes PISA to underestimate the flow[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). In secondary MR, an EROA ≥0.30 cm² by 2D PISA may still indicate severe regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29); the EACVI accepts an EROA ≥0.30 cm² or a regurgitant volume ≥45 mL as severe[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65).
- When regurgitation occupies only part of systole, the regurgitant volume should be used, because a measurement from a single frame overestimates the EROA[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).

### Pitfalls

- A normal LA size generally excludes severe chronic MR, whereas a dilated LA alone does not establish it[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
- Color jet area is useful to exclude MR but is unreliable for grading its severity; for the assessment of regurgitant jets, the color scale should be set to 50 to 70 cm/s[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29) ( [chapter 03](https://intro2echo.com/shift)). The figure shows the flow convergence at an aliasing velocity of 32 cm/s. Its jet area is the value at a color scale of 50 to 70 cm/s.
- Systolic flow reversal in the pulmonary veins is specific for severe regurgitation but is not sensitive[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).

### Key points

1. Four or more specific criteria for mild or for severe MR define the grade; otherwise, the EROA, the regurgitant volume and the regurgitant fraction decide.
2. Mitral regurgitation is severe when the EROA is ≥0.40 cm², the regurgitant volume is ≥60 mL and the regurgitant fraction is ≥50%.
3. A mitral E velocity >1.2 m/s and systolic flow reversal in the pulmonary veins support a severe grade.
4. An LVEF ≤60% or an LV end-systolic diameter ≥40 mm indicates ACC/AHA stage C2.

16

## Tricuspid regurgitation

Right heart volume overload

Tricuspid regurgitation imposes a volume overload on the right heart. The RV, the tricuspid annulus and the RA dilate, and the systemic veins distend. In torrential regurgitation, the jet velocity falls because the RV and RA pressures approach each other.

Of the 7 specific criteria for severe TR, 6 are present, a majority[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The peak TR velocity of 3.2 m/s gives an RV systolic pressure of 56 mmHg.

### Echocardiographic findings by grade

1. In **mild** regurgitation, the jet is small, narrow and central, the CW Doppler signal is faint, hepatic vein flow is systolic-dominant and the IVC diameter is below 2 cm[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29); the RA pressure estimate uses 2.1 cm ( [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure)).
2. In **moderate** regurgitation, the RV and RA are normal in size or mildly dilated, hepatic vein flow shows systolic blunting, the vena contracta is 0.3–0.69 cm and the EROA is 0.20–0.39 cm²[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
3. In **severe** regurgitation, the vena contracta is ≥0.7 cm, the EROA ≥0.40 cm², the regurgitant volume ≥45 mL, the PISA radius >0.9 cm (with the color baseline shifted to an aliasing velocity of 28 cm/s) and the jet area >10 cm²; hepatic vein flow shows systolic reversal, the tricuspid E velocity is >1.0 m/s and the IVC diameter is >2.5 cm[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The ACC/AHA criteria add a dilated RV and RA and an elevated RA pressure with a c-V wave[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).
4. Two grades beyond severe are defined: **massive** regurgitation, with a vena contracta of 1.4–2.0 cm, an EROA of 0.60–0.79 cm² and a regurgitant volume of 60–74 mL, and **torrential** regurgitation, with a vena contracta ≥2.1 cm, an EROA ≥0.80 cm² and a regurgitant volume ≥75 mL[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65). An echocardiography report may describe massive and torrential TR as subgroups of severe TR[36](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-36).

### Integrated grading

The ASE algorithm first asks whether most of the specific criteria for mild or for severe TR are present[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The guideline text asks for a majority of the specific signs, so the diagram counts four of the seven. If neither set has a majority, the TR is probably moderate, and the vena contracta, the EROA and the regurgitant volume decide between mild, moderate and severe[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). Unlike MR and AR, TR has no validated quantitative grades I to IV, and clinical experience with its quantitation is limited[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). If the findings are inconsistent, the grade is indeterminate, and TEE or CMR can quantify the regurgitation[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The algorithm gives no rule for discordant quantitative measures; the diagram uses the middle grade of the three. It uses the same rule for the EACVI grades beyond severe[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65).

### Pitfalls

- The peak TR velocity does not indicate the regurgitant volume. Very severe TR often has a low peak velocity, about 2 m/s, because the RV and RA pressures equalize[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29); the EACVI lists a peak velocity below 2 m/s for massive TR[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65). A triangular jet signal then underestimates the RV systolic pressure[32](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-32) ( [chapter 07](https://intro2echo.com/pressure)).
- Systolic flow reversal in the hepatic veins is specific for severe TR once other causes (atrial fibrillation, pacing, heart block, respiration, preload, altered RA or RV compliance) have been excluded[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29).
- The regurgitant volume threshold for severe TR (45 mL) is lower than that for severe MR (60 mL) at the same EROA, because the TR velocity is lower[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). The regurgitant volume is the EROA × the VTI of the TR jet, and a slow jet has a short VTI. At a peak velocity of 2.2 m/s, an EROA of 0.80 cm² gives about 40 mL; in the figure, the vena contracta and the EROA reach the grades for massive and torrential TR before the regurgitant volume does[65](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-65).
- The figure shows the flow convergence at an aliasing velocity of 28 cm/s, and the jet as a share of the RA area at a scale of 50 to 70 cm/s, at which the jet criteria are defined[29](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-29). By the PISA method ( [chapter 15](https://intro2echo.com/mr)), the flow rate 2πr² × 28 cm/s equals the EROA × the peak TR velocity. Beyond severe TR the velocity falls as the regurgitation increases, so the PISA radius stays at 1.0–1.1 cm while the EROA rises from 0.60 to 1.21 cm².
- At the time of left-sided valve surgery, tricuspid repair is reasonable when the tricuspid annulus measures more than 4.0 cm in diastole, even with less than severe TR[38](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-38).

### Key points

1. Four or more of the seven specific criteria for mild or for severe TR define the grade; otherwise, the vena contracta, the EROA and the regurgitant volume decide.
2. Tricuspid regurgitation is severe when the vena contracta is ≥0.7 cm, the EROA is ≥0.40 cm² and the regurgitant volume is ≥45 mL.
3. Systolic flow reversal in the hepatic veins is specific for severe tricuspid regurgitation.
4. A low peak TR velocity does not exclude severe tricuspid regurgitation.

B6

## Prosthetic valves

Aortic prostheses: stenosis and mismatch

A prosthetic aortic valve is assessed by its Doppler velocities, its EOA and its DVI, against the reference values of its type and size. A high gradient can come from obstruction of the valve, from a normal valve that is small for the patient, or from a high flow.

**Possible stenosis of the bioprosthesis.** The peak velocity is 3.3 m/s, the mean gradient 24 mmHg and the DVI 0.29. The EOA of 1.12 cm² is more than 1 SD below the reference of 1.6 ± 0.3 cm² for this valve[71](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-71). The jet peaks 96 ms after the onset of flow, at an AT/ET of 0.33: a triangular to intermediate contour[71](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-71). The leaflets are mildly thickened and open less. Mild thickening is often the first sign of primary failure of a biological valve, and it calls for a shorter interval between studies[71](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-71). At least one measure is outside its normal range. Significant stenosis needs a flow-dependent and a flow-independent measure that both suggest it[71](https://intro2echo.com/#ref-71).
